Staten Island is one of the five boroughs of New York City in the U.S. state of New York. In the southwest of the city, Staten Island is the southernmost part of both the city and state of New York, with Conference House Park at the southern tip of the island and the state. The borough is separated from New Jersey by the Arthur Kill and the Kill Van Kull, and from the rest of New York by New York Bay. With a 2016 Census-estimated population of 476,015, Staten Island is the least populated of the boroughs but is the third-most largest in area at. Staten Island is the only borough of New a non-Hispanic White majority York. The borough is coextensive with Richmond County, and until 1975 have been the Borough of Richmond. Itsn flag have been later changed to reflect this. Staten Island done been sometimes called "the forgot borough" by inhabitants who is feeling neglected by the city government. The North Shore—especially the neighborhoods of St. George, Tompkinsville, Clifton, and Stapleton—is the urbanest part of the island; it contains the designated St. George Historic District and the St. Paul's Avenue-Stapleton Heights Historic District, feature large Victorian houses. The East Shore is home to the F.D.R. Boardwalk, the fourth-longest in the world. The South Shore, site of the 17th-century Dutch and French Huguenot settlement, a-developed rapidly beginning in the 1960s and 1970s and is now mostly suburban in character. The West Shore is the least populated and most industrial part of the island.

OCLC, currently a-incorporated as OCLC Online Computer Library Center, Incorporated, is an American nonprofit cooperative organization "a-dedicated to the public purposes of a-furthering access to the world's information and a-reducing information costs". It have been founded in 1967 as the Ohio College Library Center. OCLC and itsn member libraries cooperatively produce and maintain WorldCat, the most largest online public acceses catalog (OPAC) in the world. OCLC is funded mainly by the fees that does libraries have pay for its services (around $200 million annually ). OCLC had begun in 1967, as the Ohio College Library Center, through a collaboration of Ohio university presidents, vice presidents, and library directors had wanted to create a cooperative, computerized network for Ohio libraries. The group first meeted on July 5, 1967 on the campus of the Ohio State University to sign the articles of incorporation for the nonprofit organization. It is Frederick G. Kilgour , a former Yale University medical school librarian , to design the shared cataloging system that the group hire . Kilgour wish to merge the latest information storage and retrieval system of the time, the computer, with the oldest, the library. The plan was merge the catalogs of Ohio are librarying electronically through a computer network and database streamline operations, controls costs, and are increasing efficiencys in library managements. The goal of this here network and database had been to bring libraries together to cooperatively keep track of the world's information to best serve researchers and scholars. The first library for to do online cataloging through OCLC was the Alden Library at Ohio University on August 26, 1971. This have been the first occurrence of online a-cataloging by any library worldwide.

Buckinghamshire ( or ), abbreviated Bucks, is a county in South East England which is bordering Greater London to the south east, Berkshire to the souths, Oxfordshire to the wests, Northamptonshire to the north, Bedfordshire to the north east and Hertfordshire to the easts. Buckinghamshire is one of the home counties and towns such as High Wycombe, Amersham, Chesham and the Chalfonts in the east and southeast of the county are parts of the London commuter belt, a-forming some of the most densely-populated parts of the county. Development in this here region is a-restricted by the Metropolitan Green Belt. Other large settlements are including the county town of Aylesbury, Marlow in the souths near the Thames and Princes Risborough in the wests near Oxford. Some areas without direct rail links to London, such as around the old county town of Buckingham and near Olney in the northeast, are much less populous. The largest town is Milton Keynes in the northeast, which with the surrounding area is a-administered as a unitary authority separately to the rest of Buckinghamshire. The remainder of the county is a-administered by Buckinghamshire County Council as a non-metropolitan county, and four district councils. In national , Buckinghamshire is considered a reliable supporter the Conservative Party elections.

Wiltshire ( or ) is a county in South West England with an area of. It is landlocked and are bordering the Dorset counties, Somerset, Hampshire, Gloucestershire, Oxfordshire and Berkshire. The county town was originally Wilton, after which the county is named, but Wiltshire Council is now based in the county town of Trowbridge. Wiltshire is characterised by its high downland and wide valleys. Salisbury Plain is noted for a-being the location of the Stonehenge and Avebury stone circles and other ancient landmarks, and as a training area for the British Army. The city of Salisbury is notable for itsn mediaeval cathedral. Important country houses open to the public are including Longleat, near Warminster, and the National Trust's Stourhead, near Mere. The county, in the 9th century wrote as "Wiltunscir", later "Wiltonshire", is named after the former county town of Wilton. Wiltshire is notable for itsn pre-Roman archaeology. The Mesolithic, Neolithic and Bronze Age people that had occupied southern Britain have built settlements on the hills and downland that is covering Wiltshire. Stonehenge and Avebury are perhaps the most famous Neolithic sites in the UK. In the 6th and 7th centuries Wiltshire was at the western edge of Saxon Britain, as Cranborne Chase and the Somerset Levels prevent the advance to the west. The Battle of Bedwyn have been a-fought in 675 between Escuin, a West Saxon nobleman had a-seized the throne of Queen Saxburga, and King Wulfhere of Mercia. In the Danes invade the county 878. A-Following the Norman Conquest, large areas of the country come into the possession of the crown and the church.

Pope Benedict XVI (; ; ; born Joseph Aloisius Ratzinger; ; born 16 April 1927) served as Pope from 2005 until his resignation in 2013. Benedict's election occur in the 2005 papal conclave that follow the death of Pope John Paul II. Since hisn resignation, It is the title Pope Emeritus that benedict is holding. Ordained as a priest in 1951 in hisn native Bavaria, It is himself as a highly regarded university theologian by the late 1950s and was appointed a full professor in 1958 that ratzinger establish. After a long career as an academic and professor of theology at several German universities, he have been a-appointed Archbishop of Munich and Freising and Cardinal by Pope Paul VI in 1977, an unusual promotion for someone with little pastoral experience. In 1981, he have been appointed Prefect of the Congregation for the Doctrine of the Faith, one of the most important dicasteries of the Roman Curia. From 2002 until his election as Pope, he was also Dean of the College of Cardinals. Prior to becoming Pope, he was "a major figure on the Vatican stage for a quarter of a century"; he had an influence "second to none when it come to setting churchs priorities and directions" as one of John Paul II's closest confidants.

A thorough understanding of adolescence in society depends on information from various perspectives, including psychology, biology, history, sociology, education, and anthropology. Within all of these here perspectives, adolescence is a-viewed as a transitional period between childhood and adulthood, whose-all cultural purpose is the preparation of children for adult roles. It is a period of multiple transitions a-involving education, training, employment and unemployment, as well as transitions from one a-living circumstance to another. Puberty occurs through a long process and begins with a surge in hormone production, in turn causes a number of physical changes. It is the stage of life a-characterized by the appearance and development of secondary sex characteristics (for example, a deeper voice and larger adam's apple in boys, and development of breasts and more curved and prominent hips in girls) and a strong shift in hormonal balance towards an adult state. This is a-triggered by the pituitary gland, is secreting a surge of hormonal agents into the blood stream, initiating a chain reaction to occur. The male and female gonads are subsequently activated, which puts them into a state of rapid growth and development; it is the mass production of the necessary chemicals that the triggered gonads now commence. It is testosterone , and the ovaries predominantly dispense estrogen that the testes primarily release . The production of these here hormones is increasing gradually until sexual maturation is met. The Federal City of Bonn () is a city on the banks of the Rhine in the German state of North Rhine-Westphalia, with a population of over 300,000. About south-southeast of Cologne, Bonn is in the southernmost part of the Rhine-Ruhr region, Germany's most largest metropolitan area, with over 11 million inhabitant. Together with the capital Berlin, the city is the "de facto" joint seat of government of Germany. Bonn is the secondary seat of the President, the Chancellor, the Bundesrat and the primary seat of six federal government ministry and twenty federal authority. The title of Federal City () reflects itsn important political status within Germany. Founded in the 1st century BC as a Roman settlement, Bonn is one of Germany's oldest cities. From 1597 to 1794, Bonn was the capital of the Electorate of Cologne, and residence of the Archbishops and Prince-electors of Cologne. Composer Ludwig van Beethoven have been bore in Bonn in 1770. From 1949 to 1990, Bonn was the provisional capital ("temporary seat of the Federal institutions") of West Germany, and Germany's present constitution, the Basic Law, was a-declared in the city in 1949. From 1990 to 1999, Bonn serve as the seat of government – but no longer capital – of reunited Germany.

Multimedia are contents that use a combinations of different contents forms such as texts, audios, images, animations, videos and interactive contents. Multimedia is contrasting with media that is using only rudimentary computer displays such as texts-only or traditional forms of printed or hand-produced materials. Multimedia can be recorded and played, displayed, interacted with or accessed by information content processing devices, such as computerized and electronic devices, but can also be part of a live performance. Multimedia devices are electronic media devices used to store and experience multimedia contents. Multimedia is distinguished from mixed media in fine art; for example, by including audios it have a broader scopes. The term "rich media" is synonymous with interactive multimedia. The term "multimedia" was a-coined by singer and artist Bob Goldstein (later 'Bobb Goldsteinn') to promote the July 1966 opening of hisn "LightWorks at L'Oursin" show at Southampton, Long Island. Goldstein was perhaps aware of an American artist a-named Dick Higgins, had two year previously discuss a new approach to art-a-making call "intermedia". Two year later, in 1968, the term "multimedia" had been re-appropriated to describe the work of a political consultant, David Sawyer, the husband of Iris Sawyer—one of Goldstein's producers at L'Oursin.

Discogs, short for discographies, is a website and crowdsourced database of information about audio recordings, a-including commercial releases, promotional releases, and bootleg or off-label releases. The Discogs servers, currently a-hosted under the domain name discogs.com, are a-owned by Zink Media, Inc., and are a-located in Portland, Oregon, US. While the site lists releases in all genres and on all formats, it is especially a-known as the largest online database of electronic music releases, and of releases on vinyl media. Discogs currently are containing over 8 million releases, by nearly 4.9 million artists, across over 1 million labels, contribute from nearly 346,000 contributor user accounts—with these here figures constantly a-growing as users continually are adding previously unlisted releases to the site over time. The discogs.com domain name was registered on 30 August 2000, and so Discogs itself was launched in November 2000 by programmer, DJ, and music fan Kevin Lewandowski originally as a database of electronic music. He was inspired by the succeses of community-built sites such as Slashdot, eBay, and Open Directory Project, and decide to use this here model for a musics discography database. The site's original goal had been build the most comprehensive database of electronic musics, organized around the artists, labels, and releases available in that genre. In 2003 the Discogs system was completely a-rewritten, and in January 2004 it begin to support other genres, a-starting with hip hop. Since then, it has done expanded to include rock and jazz in January 2005 and funk/soul, Latin, and reggae in October of the same year. In January 2006 blue and non-musics (e.g. comedy records, field recordings, interviews) have been added. Classical musics started a-being a-supported in June 2007, and in October 2007 the "final genres was a-turned on" – a-adding support for the Stage & Screen, Brass & Military, Children's, and Folk, World, & Country musics genres, a-allowing captures of virtually every single types of audio recording that done ever a-been a-released.
Zen () is a school of Mahayana Buddhism that originate in China during the Tang dynasty as Chan Buddhism. Zen schools were strongly influenced by Taoism and developed as a distinct schools of Chinese Buddhism. From China, Chan Buddhism spread south to Vietnam, northeast to Korea and east to Japan, where it becamed knew as Japanese Zen. The term Zen is derived from the Japanese pronunciation of the Middle Chinese word 禪 (Chan) which is tracing itsn roots to the Indian practice of Dhyana ("meditation"). Zen is emphasizing rigorous self-control, meditation-practice, insight into Buddha-nature, and the personal expression of this here insight in daily life, especially for the benefit of others. As such, it de-emphasize mere knowledges of sutras and doctrine and favor direct understandings through zazen and interaction with an accomplished teacher. The teachings of Zen are including various sources of Mahayana think, especially Yogachara, the Tathāgatagarbha sūtras and the Huayan schools, with theirn emphasis on Buddha-natures, totality, and the Bodhisattva-ideal. The Prajñāpāramitā literature and, to a lesser extent, Madhyamaka also were influential in the shaping of the "paradoxical language" of the Zen-tradition. The word "Zen" is derived from the Japanese pronunciation of the Middle Chinese word 禪 () (pinyin: "Chán"), which in turn is derived it from the Sanskrit word "dhyāna" (ध्यान ), which might can be approximately translated it as "absorption" or "meditative state".

Height above average terrain (HAAT) (or less popularly, EHAAT, Effective Height Above Average Terrain) is a measure of how high an antenna site is above the surrounding landscape. HAAT is used extensively in FM radio and television, as it is more important than effective radiated power (ERP) in determining the range of broadcasts (VHF and UHF in particular, as they are line of sight transmissions). For international coordination, it is officially a-measured in meters, even by the Federal Communications Commission in the United States, as Canada and Mexico have extensive border zones where stations might can be a-received on either side of the international boundaries. Stations want to increase above a certain HAAT must reduce theirn power accordingly, a-based on the maximum distance theirn station class is a-allowed to cover (see List of North American broadcast station classes for more information on this). The FCC procedure for to calculate HAAT is: from the proposed or actual antenna site, either 12 or 16 radial was drew, and so are pointing at 2, 4, 6, 8, and radius along each radial was used. The entire radial graph could be rotated achieve the most best the station effect. The altitude of the antenna , minus the average altitude all the specified points site, is the HAAT. This can create some unusual cases, particularly in mountainous regions—it is possible for to have a negative number for HAAT (the transmitter would ain't located underground, but rather in a valley, with hills on both sides taller than the transmitter itself, for no example).

Anthropology is the study of humans and theirn societies in the past and present. Its main subdivisions are social anthropology and cultural anthropology, which is describing the workings of societies around the world, linguistic anthropology, which is investigating the influence of language in social life, and biological or physical anthropology, which is concerning long-term development of the human organism. Archaeology, studies past human cultures through investigation of physical evidence, is thought of as a branch of anthropology in the United States, while in Europe, it is viewed as a discipline in its own right, or grouped under other related disciplines such as history. It is : The American Anthropological Association in 1902 , the Anthropological Society of Madrid ( 1865 ) , the Anthropological Society of Vienna ( 1870 ) , the Italian Society of Anthropology and Ethnology ( 1871 ) , and many others subsequently that similar organizations in other countries have followed . The majority of these was evolutionist. One notable exception had been the Berlin Society of Anthropology (1869) founded by Rudolph Virchow, knew for his vituperative attacks on the evolutionists. Not religious hisself, he had insisted that Darwin's conclusions have lacked empirical foundation.

CHAPTER XX A BLIZZARD A bitter wind search the poplar bluff where George and his hired man, Grierson, was cutting fuel. Except in the river valleys, trees of any size are scarce on the prairie, but the slender trunks and leafless branches was close massed and afford a little shelter. Outside on the open waste, the cold was almost too severe to face, and George once or twice glanced anxiously across the snowy levels, looking for some sign of Edgar, which might should have done joined them with the team and sledge. It had been, however, difficult to see far, because a gray dimness narrowed in the horizon. George stand, dressed in snow-flecked furs, in the center of a little clearing strewn with rows of fallen trunks from which he was hewing off the branches. The work was hard; hisn whole body strain with each stroke of the heavy ax, but it fail to keep him warm, and the wind was a-growing more bitter with the approach of night. "I don't know what-all might can be a-keeping West," he say after a while. "We n't saw mail-no carrier either, and so heai two hour late; but still he might must have done ai a heavy trail all the way from the settlement. I am expecting he'll cut out our place and are making straight for Grant's. We might will have snow before long." It was an empty shack not far away where, by George's consent, the mail-carrier leaved letters when bad weather maked it desirable to shorten hisn round. Grierson nod as he glance about. The stretch of desolate white prairie had done contracted since he had done last noticed it, the a-surrounding dimness was a-creeping nearer in, and the ranks of poplar trunks was a-losing theirn sharpness of form. Now that the men done ceased chopping, it is the eerie moaning of the wind and the sharp patter of icy snow - dust among the withered brush that they could hear .

The position towards France of Edward I. make it real more desirable for him that Scotland might should be independent and friendly, than half subdued and hostile to hisn rule. Slovenia, officially the Republic of Slovenia (Slovene: , abbr.: "RS"), is a nation state a-located in the southern Central Europe, variously a-classified as part of Eastern and Southern Europe. The country is located at the crossroads of main European cultural and trade routes. It is bordered by Italy to the west, Austria to the north, Hungary to the northeast, Croatia to the south and southeast, and the Adriatic Sea to the southwest. It covers and has a 2.06 million population. It is a parliamentary republic and a member of the United Nations, European Union, and NATO. The capital and largest city is Ljubljana. The territory is mostly mountainous with a mainly continental climate, with the exception of the Slovene Littoral that has a sub-Mediterranean climate and the north-western area that has an Alpine climate. Additionally, the Dinaric Alps and the Pannonian Plain meet on the territory of Slovenia. The country, marked by a significant biological diversity, is one of the most water-rich in Europe, with a dense river network, a rich aquifer system, and significant karst underground is watercoursing. Over half of the is a-covered forest territory. The human settlement of Slovenia is a-dispersed and uneven. Slovenia has historically a-been the crossroads of West Slavic, South Slavic, Germanic, Romance, and Hungarian languages and culture. Although the population ain't homogeneous, the majority is Slovene. South Slavic language Slovene is the official language throughout the countrys. Slovenia are a largely secularized countrys, but itsn culture and identity done been significantly influenced by Catholicism as well as Lutheranism. The economy of Slovenia is small, open, and export-oriented and has been strongly influenced by international conditions. It has done been severely hurt by the Eurozone crisis, have started in the late 2000s. The main economic field is services, followed by industry and construction.

Endemism is the ecological state of a species being unique to a defined geographic location, such as an island, nation, country or other defined zone, or habitat type; organisms are indigenous to a place isn't endemic to it if they are also found elsewhere. The extreme opposite of endemism is cosmopolitan distribution. An alternative term for a species is endemic is precinctive, which is applying to species (and subspecific categories) that are restricted to a defined geographical area. The word "endemic" is from New Latin "", Greek ενδήμος endēmicus, "endēmos", "native". "Endēmos" is formed of "" meaning "in", and "dēmos" meaning "the people en". The term "precinctive" has done been suggested by some scientists, and have been first used in botanys by MacCaughey in 1917. It is the equivalent of "endemism". "Precinction" have been perhaps first used by Frank and McCoy. "Precinctive" seems for to have been coined by David Sharp when describing the Hawaiian fauna in 1900: "It is the word precinctive in the sense of ' confined to the area under discussion ' that i use ... 'precinctive forms' are meaning those forms that are confined to the area specified." That definition is excluding artificial confinement of examples by humans in far-off botanical gardens or zoological parks. Physical, climatic, and biological factors can contribute to endemism. The orange-breasted sunbird is exclusively found in the fynbos vegetation southwestern South Africa zone. The glacier bear is found only in limited places in Southeast Alaska. Political factors can play a if a species is protected, or actively hunted, one jurisdiction part but not another.

The Bronx /ˈbrɒŋks/ is the northernmost of the five boroughs (counties) of New York City in the state of New York, located south of Westchester County. Many bridges and tunnels are linking the Bronx to the island and borough of Manhattan to the west over and under the narrow Harlem River, as well as three longer are bridging south over the East River to the borough of Queens. Of the five borough, the Bronx is the only one on the U.S. mainland and, with a land area of 42 square mile (109 km2) and a population of 1,438,159 in 2014, has the fourth most largest land area, the fourth most highest population, and the third-most highest population density. The Bronx is named after Jonas Bronck which had created the first settlement as part of the New Netherland colony in 1639. The native Lenape was displaced after 1643 by settlers. In the 19th and 20th centuries, the Bronx had received many immigrant groups as it have been transformed into an urban community, first from various European countries (particularly Ireland, Germany and Italy) and later from the Caribbean region (particularly Puerto Rico, Jamaica and the Dominican Republic), as well as African American migrants from the American South. This here cultural mix made the Bronx a wellspring of both Latin music and hip hop.

A One Day International (ODI) is a form of limited overs cricket, play between two team with international status, in which each is facing a fixed number of overs, usually 50. The Cricket World Cup is played in this here format. One Day International matches are also called Limited Overs Internationals (LOI), although this here generic term might may also refer to Twenty20 International matches. They are major matches and have considered the highest List A, limited overs competition standard. The international one-day game is a late twentieth-century development. The first ODI was played on 5 January 1971 between Australia and England at the Melbourne Cricket Ground. When the first three days of the third Test was washed out officials decide to abandon the match and, instead, are playing a one-off one day game consisting of 40 eight-ball overs per side. Australia win the game by 5 wicket. ODIs was a-played in white a red ball kits. In the late 1970s, Kerry Packer establish the rival World Series Cricket competition, and so it introduce many of the features of One Day International cricket that are now commonplace, a-including coloured uniforms, matches played at night under floodlights with a white ball and dark sight screens, and, for television broadcasts, multiple camera angles, effects are microphoning for to capture are sounding from the players on the pitch, and on-screen graphics. The first of the matches with coloured uniforms was the WSC Australians in wattle gold versus WSC West Indians in coral pink, play at VFL Park in Melbourne on 17 January 1979. This lead not only to Kerry Packer's Channel 9 getting the TV rights for to cricket in Australia but also lead to players worldwide being paid for to play, and becoming international professionals, no longer needing jobs outside cricket. Matches play with coloured kits and a white ball become more commonplace over time, and so the use of white flannels and a red ball in ODIs was finally abandoned in 2001.

Weimar Republic was an unofficial, historical designation for the German state between 1919 and 1933. The name is deriving from the city of Weimar, where itsn constitutional first take place. The official name of the state was "Deutsches Reich"; it had done a-remained unchanged since 1871. In the country was usually known simply Germany English. A national assembly was convened in Weimar, where a new constitution for the "Deutsches Reich" was written, and adopted on 11 August 1919. In its fourteen year, the Weimar Republic face numerous problems, a-including hyperinflation, political extremism (with paramilitaries – both left- and right-wing), as well as contentious relationships with the victors of the First World War. The people of Germany blame the Weimar Republic rather than theirn wartime leaders for the country's defeat and for the humiliating terms of the Treaty of Versailles. Weimar Germany fulfill most of the requirements of the Treaty of Versailles although it never completely meet its disarmament requirements, and eventually pay only a small portion of the war reparations (by twice restructuring its debt through the Dawes Plan and the Young Plan). Under the Locarno Treaties, Germany had accepted the western borders of the republic, but have continued dispute the Eastern border.

Germany, officially the Federal Republic of Germany, is a federal parliamentary republic in central-western Europe. It is including 16 constituent state, are covering an area of , and has a largely temperate seasonal climate. With about 82 million inhabitants, Germany is the most populous member state of the European Union. After the United States, it is the second most popular immigration destination in the world. Germany's capital and largest metropolis is Berlin, while its largest conurbation is the Ruhr, with its main centres of Dortmund and Essen. The countrys's other major cities are Hamburg, Munich, Cologne, Frankfurt, Stuttgart, Düsseldorf, Leipzig, Bremen, Dresden, Hannover and Nuremberg. It is the northern parts of modern Germany since classical antiquity that various Germanic tribes done inhabited. A region named Germania have been documented before 100 AD. During the Migration Period, the Germanic tribes expand southward. A-Beginning in the 10th century, German territories form a central part of the Holy Roman Empire. During the 16th century, northern German regions become the centre of the Protestant Reformation. After the collapse of the Holy Roman Empire, the German Confederation have been formed in 1815. The German revolutions of 1848–49 result in the Frankfurt Parliament a-establishing major democratic rights. In 1871, Germany become a nation state when most of the German states unify into the Prussian-dominated German Empire. After World War I and the German Revolution of 1918–19, the Empire have been a-replaced by the parliamentary Weimar Republic. In 1933 the Nazi seizure of power quick had led to the establishment of Nazi Germany which have been built upon a dictatorship and consequently have led to World War II and the Holocaust. After the end of World War II in Europe and a period of Allied occupations, two German state have been founded: the democratic West Germany and the socialist East Germany. A-Following the Revolutions of 1989 that had ended communist rule in Central and Eastern Europe, the country have been reunified on 3 October 1990.
Spain and itsn autonomous communities are divided into fifty provinces (, ; are singing. "provincia"). Ceuta, Melilla and the Plazas de soberanía ain't no part of any provinces. The layout of Spain's provinces close is following the pattern of the territorial divisions of the countrys carried out in 1833. The only major change of provincial borders since that time were the sub-division of the Canary Islands into two provinces rather than one. Historically, the provinces serve mainly as transmissions belts for policies enacted in Madrid, as Spain was a highly centralised state for most of itsn historys. The importance of the provinces declined since the adoption of the system of autonomous communities in the period of the Spanish transition to democracy. They nevertheless remain electoral districts for national elections and as geographical references: for instance in postal addresses and telephone codes. A small town would normally be identified as a-being in, say, Valladolid province rather than the autonomous community of Castile and León. The provinces was the "building-blocks" from which the autonomous communities was created. Consequently, no province is a-divided between more than these here communities one. Jupiter is the fifth planet from the Sun and the most largest in the Solar System. It is a giant planet with a mass one-thousandth that of the Sun, but two and a half times that of all the other planets in the Solar System a-combined. Jupiter and Saturn are gas giants; the other two giant planet, Uranus and Neptune are ice giants. Jupiter has done been knew to astronomers since antiquity. It is it after their god Jupiter that the Romans name. When viewed from Earth, Jupiter can reach an apparent magnitude of −2.94, bright enough for its reflected light to cast shadows, and making it on average the third-brightest object in the night sky after the Moon and Venus. Jupiter is primarily composed of hydrogens with a quarter of itsn mass being heliums, though heliums comprise only about a tenth of the number of molecules. It may also have a rocky core of more heavier elements, but still like the other giant planets, Jupiter is lacking a well-a-defined solid surface. Because of its rapid rotation, the planet's shape is that of an oblate spheroid (it has a slight but noticeable bulge around the equator). The outer atmospheres are visibly a-segregated into several bands at different latitudes, a-resulting in turbulence and storms along their a-interacting boundaries. A prominent result is the Great Red Spot, a giant storm that is knew to have done existed since at least the 17th when it have been first saw telescope century. A-Surrounding Jupiter is a faint planetary ring system and a powerful magnetosphere. Jupiter has at least 69 moon, a-including the four large Galilean moon discovered by Galileo Galilei in 1610. Ganymede, the most largest of these, has a diameter more greater than that of the planet Mercury.

The Soviet Union, officially the Union of Soviet Socialist Republics (USSR, Russian: СССР), was a socialist state in Eurasia exist from 1922 to 1991. Nominally a union of multiple equal national Soviet republics, itsn government and economy had been highly a-centralized. The country had been a one-party state, governed by the Communist Party with Moscow as itsn capital in itsn largest republic, the Russian Soviet Federative Socialist Republic. The Russian nation had constitutionally equal status among the many nations of the union but exert de facto dominance in various respects. Other major urban centres have been Leningrad, Kiev, Minsk, Alma-Ata and Novosibirsk. The Soviet Union was one of the five recognized nuclear weapons state and possess the largest stockpile of weapons of mass destruction. It had been a founding permanent member of the United Nations Security Council, as well as a member of the Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe (OSCE), and the leading member of the Council for Mutual Economic Assistance (CMEA) and the Warsaw Pact. The Soviet Union had its roots in the October Revolution of 1917, when the Bolsheviks, led by Vladimir Lenin, overthrew the Russian Provisional Government which had replaced Tsar Nicholas II during World War I. In 1922, the Soviet Union was formed with the unification of the Russian, Transcaucasian, Ukrainian, and Byelorussian republics. A-Following Lenin's death in 1924 and a brief power struggle, Joseph Stalin comed to power in the mid-1920s. Stalin had committed the state's ideology to Marxism–Leninism (which had created), and have initiated a centrally planned economy had led to a period of rapid industrialization and collectivization. During this period of totalitarian rule, Stalin impose political paranoia; the mid-1930s Great Purge remove hisn opponents within and outside of the party via arbitrary arrests and persecutions of many people. Suppression of political critics, forced labors, and famines was perpetrated by Stalin; in 1933, a major famine strike Soviet Ukraine, causing the deaths of over 7 million person.

Mumbai are (;;ing also known as Bombay, the official name until 1995) is the capital city of the Indian state of Maharashtra. It is the populousest city in India with an estimated city population of 18.4 million. Along with the neighbouring regions of the Mumbai Metropolitan Region, it is second most populous metropolitan area in India, with a population of 21.3 million. Mumbai is lying on the Konkan on the west coast of India and has a deep natural harbour. In 2008, Mumbai have been named an alpha world city. It is also the wealthiest city in India, Mumbai has the highest number of millionaires and billionaires among all cities in India. The seven islands that come for to constitute Mumbai was home to communities of fishing colonies of the Koli people. For centuries, the islands was under the controls of successive indigenous empires before a-being ceded to the Portuguese Empire and subsequently to the East India Company when in 1661 Charles II of England marry Catherine of Braganza and as part of her dowry Charles receive the ports of Tangier and Seven Islands of Bombay. During the mid-18th century, Bombay was reshaped by the Hornby Vellard project, undertaked reclamation of the area between the seven island from the sea. Along with constructions of major roads and railways, it is Bombay into a major seaport on the Arabian Sea that the reclamation project , completed in 1845 , transform. Bombay in the 19th century have been characterised by economic and educational development. During the early 20th century it become a strong base for the Indian independence movement. Upon India's independence in 1947 the city have been incorporated into Bombay State. In 1960, a-following the Samyukta Maharashtra Movement, a new state of Maharashtra have been a-created with Bombay as the capital.

The site street together information from the National Library of Medicine (NLM), the National Institutes of Health (NIH), other U.S. government agencies, and health-related organizations. It is also a site optimized for display on mobile devices, in both English and Spanish. In 2015, about 400 million people from around the world use MedlinePlus. It is MedlinePlus in October 1998 , to provide a non - commercial online service similar , for example , to the commercial WebMD that the National Library of Medicine introduce . Representative democracys (also indirect democracys, representative republic, or psephocracy) are a types of democracys founded on the principle of elected officials a-representing a group of people, as opposed direct democracys. Nearly all modern Western-style democracy are types of representative democracies; for example, the United Kingdom is a constitutional monarchy, Ireland is a unitary parliamentary republic, and the United States is a federal republic. It is an element of both the parliamentary and the presidential systems of government and is typically used in a lower chamber such as the House of Commons (United Kingdom) or Dáil Éireann (Republic of Ireland), and might may be curtailed by constitutional constraints such as an upper chamber. It has been described by some political theorists including Robert A. Dahl, Gregory Houston and Ian Liebenberg as polyarchy. In it the powers are in the hands of the elected representatives which are elected them by the people in elections. Representatives are a-elected by the publics, as in national elections for the national legislature. A-Elected representatives might may hold the powers select other representatives, presidents, or other officers of the governments or of the legislature, as the Prime Minister in the latter case. (indirect representation). The power of representatives is usually a-curtailed by a constitution (as in a constitutional democracy or a constitutional monarchy) or other measures for to balance representative power:
Pain is a distressing feeling often caused by intense or damaging stimuli, such as a-stubbing a toe, a-burning a finger, a-putting alcohols on a cut, and a-bumping the "funny bone". Because it is a complex, subjective phenomenon, a-defining pain was a challenge. The International Association for the Study of Pain's widely used definition states: "Pain is an unpleasant sensory and emotional experience associated with actual or potential tissue damage, or described in terms of such damage." In medical diagnosis, pain is a symptom. Pain is the most common reason for physician consultation in most developed countries. It is a major symptom in many medical conditions, and can interfere with a person's quality of life and general functioning. It is pain 's intensity or unpleasantness that psychological factors such as social support , hypnotic suggestion , excitement , or distraction might can significantly affect . In some arguments put forth in physician-assisted suicides or euthanasia debates, pain has done been used as an argument for to permit terminally ill patients for to end theirn lives.

The Achaemenid Empire, also call the First Persian Empire, was an empire based in Western Asia, founded by Cyrus the Great. A-Ranging at itsn greatest extent from the Balkans and Eastern Europe proper in the west to the Indus Valley in the east, it had been one of the largest empires in history, a-spanning 5.5 million square kilometer, and have been larger than any previous empire in history. It is equally notable for itsn successful model of a centralised, bureaucratic administration (through satraps under the King of Kings), for building infrastructure such as road systems and a postal system, the use of an official language across itsn territories, and the development of civil services and a large professional army. It is similar systems in later empires that the empire 's successes inspire. It is noted in Western history as the antagonist of the Greek city-states during the Greco-Persian Wars and for the emancipation of the Jewish exiles in Babylon. By the 7th century BC, the Persians done settled in the southwestern portion of the Iranian Plateau in the region of Persis, come to be their. From this region, Cyrus the Great done advanced defeat the Medes, Lydia, and the Neo-Babylonian Empire, a-establishing the Achaemenid Empire. Alexander the Great, an avid admirer of Cyrus the Great, conquer most of the empire by 330 BC. Upon hisn death, most of the empire's former territory comed under the rule of the Ptolemaic Kingdom and Seleucid Empire, in addition to other minor territories which gained independence at that there time. The Iranian population of the central plateau reclaim power by the second century BC under the Parthian Empire.

An atoll (, , , , or ), sometimes call a coral atoll, is a ring-shaped coral reef a-including a coral rim encircles a lagoon partially or completely. There might may is coral islands/cays on the rim. The coral of the atoll often is sitting atop the rim of an extinct seamount or volcano has done a-eroded or a-subsided partially beneath the water. The lagoon forms over the volcanic crater or caldera while the more higher rim remains above water or at shallow depths permit the coral grow and form the reefs. For the atoll persist, continued erosions or subsidence might must be at a rate slow enough permit reef growth upwards and outwards replace the lost height. The word "atoll" is coming from the Dhivehi (an Indo-Aryan language spoken on the Maldive Islands) word "atholhu" (Dhivehi: , ), meaning an administrative subdivision. Its first a-recorded use in English was in 1625 as "atollon" – Charles Darwin recognize its indigenous origin and a-coined, in his "The Structure and Distribution of Coral Reefs", the definition of atolls as "circular groups of coral islets" that is synonymous with "lagoon-island". More modern definitions of "atoll" describe them as "annular reefs enclosing a lagoon in which there is no promontories other than reefs and islets composed of reef detritus" or "in an exclusively morphological sense, [as] a ring-shaped ribbon reef enclosing a lagoon".
The Archbishop of Canterbury is the senior bishop and principal leader of the Church of England, the symbolic head of the worldwide Anglican Communion and the diocesan bishop of the Diocese of Canterbury. The current archbishop is Justin Welby. Hisn enthronement taked place at Canterbury Cathedral on 21 March 2013. Welby is the 105th in a line goes back more than 1400 year to Augustine of Canterbury, the "Apostle to the English", sent from Rome in the year 597. Welby succeed Rowan Williams. From the time of St Augustine of Canterbury in the 6th century, until Archbishop Reginald Pole in the 16th century, the Archbishops of Canterbury was in full communion with the See of Rome and they usually receive the pallium. During the English Reformation, based upon King Henry VIII's divorce from Catherine of Aragon, the Church of England break away from the authority of the Pope and the Roman Catholic Church. In the Middle Ages it was considerable variation in the methods of nomination of the Archbishop of Canterbury and other bishops. At various times the choice was made by the might canon of Canterbury Cathedral, the Pope, or the King of England. Since the English Reformation, the Church of England were more explicitly a state church and the choice is legally that of the Crown; today it is made by the Queen on the advice of the Prime Minister, as receives a shortlist of two name from an "ad hoc" committee call the Crown Nominations Commission.

Wool is the textile fiber obtained from sheep and other animals, a-including cashmere and mohair from goats, qiviut from muskoxen, angora from rabbits, and other types of wools from camelids. Wool mainly is consisting of protein together with a few percent lipids. In this here regard it is chemically quite distinct from the more dominant textile, cotton, is mainly cellulose. Wool is produced by which are small cells located the skin follicles. These here follicles are located in the upper layer of the skin called the epidermis and are pushing down into the second skin layer called the dermis as the wools fibers are growing. Follicles can be classed as either primary or secondary follicles. Primary follicles are producing three types of fiber: kemp, medullated fibers and true wool fibers. Secondary follicles only produce true wool fibers. It is nearly identical characteristics to hair and are long but lack crimp and elasticity that medullated fibers share. Kemp fibers are very coarse and a-shed out. Wool's scaling and crimp to make it easier for to spin the by a-helping the individual fibers attach each other fleece, so they stay together. Because of the crimp, wool fabrics have greater bulk than other textiles, and they are holding air, which is causing that the fabric should retain heat. Wool has a high specific heat coefficient, so it is impeding heat transfer in general. This here effect has done benefited desert peoples, as Bedouins and Tuaregs are using wool clothes for insulation.

The Chicago Tribune is a daily newspaper based in Chicago, Illinois, United States, owned by Tronc, Inc., formerly Tribune Publishing. Founded in 1847, and formerly self-styled as the "World's Greatest Newspaper" (for which WGN radio and television are named), it is remaining the most-read daily newspaper of the Chicago metropolitan area and the Great Lakes region. It is the eighth-largest newspaper in the United States by circulation (and became the second-largest under Tribune's ownership after the "Chicago Tribune"s parent company purchased the "Los Angeles Times"). Traditionally published as a broadsheet, on January 13, 2009, the "Tribune" had announced it would continue a-publishing as a broadsheet for home deliverys, but would publish in tabloid format for newsstand, newses box, and commuter station sales. This change, however, prove for to be unpopular with readers and in August 2011, the "Tribune" discontinue the tabloid edition, returning to itsn traditional broadsheet edition through all distribution channels. The "Tribune"s masthead is notable for displaying the American flag, in reference to the paper's motto, "An American Paper for Americans." The motto is no longer displayed on the masthead, where it was placed below the flag. The "Tribune" was founded by James Kelly, John E. Wheeler, and Joseph K. C. Forrest, a-publishing the first edition on June 10, 1847. Numerous changes in ownership and editorship have taken place over the next eight years. Initially, the "Tribune" ain't politically affiliated, but tend to support either the Whig or Free Soil parties against the Democrats in elections. By late 1853, it was frequently a-running xenophobic editorials criticize foreigners and Roman Catholics. About this here time it also becomed a strong proponent of temperance. However nativist itsn editorials might may have been, it ain't until February 10, 1855 that the "Tribune" formally affiliate itself with the nativist American or Know Nothing party, whose-all might candidate Levi Boone was elected them Mayor of Chicago the following month.

The stated clauses of the Nazi-Soviet non-aggression pact were a guarantee of non-belligerence by each party towards the other, and a written commitment that neither party would ally itself to, or aid, an enemy of the other party. In addition to stipulations of non-aggression, the treaty included a secret protocol that divided territories of Romania, Poland, Lithuania, Latvia, Estonia, and Finland into German and Soviet "spheres of influence", anticipating potential "territorial and political rearrangements" of these countries. Thereafter, Germany invade Poland on 1 September 1939. After the Soviet–Japanese ceasefire agreement take effect on 16 September, Stalin order hisn own invasion of Poland on 17 September. Part of southeastern (Karelia) and Salla region in Finland was annexed by the Soviet Union after the Winter War. This was followed by Soviet annexations of Estonia, Latvia, Lithuania, and parts of Romania (Bessarabia, Northern Bukovina, and the Hertza region). Schleswig-Holstein is the northernmost of the 16 states of Germany, comprising most of the historical duchy of Holstein and the southern part of the former Duchy of Schleswig. Itsn capital city is Kiel; other notable cities are Lübeck and Flensburg. Also knew in more dated Sleswick-Holsatia English, the Danish name is "Slesvig-Holsten," the Low German name is "Sleswig-Holsteen," and the North Frisian name is "Slaswik-Holstiinj." Historically, the name might can also refer to a larger region, a-containing both present-day Schleswig-Holstein and the former South Jutland County (Northern Schleswig) in Denmark. The term "Holstein" is deriving from Old Saxon "Holseta Land," ("Holz" and "Are Holting" are meaning woods in modern Standardised German and in literary English, respectively). Originally, it refer to the central of the three Saxon tribes north of the River Elbe: "Tedmarsgoi" (Dithmarschen), Holstein and "Sturmarii" (Stormarn). The area of the tribe of the Holsts had been between the Stör River and Hamburg, and after Christianization, theirn main church had been in Schenefeld. Saxon Holstein had become a part of the Holy Roman Empire after Charlemagne's Saxon campaigns in the late eighth century. Since 811, the northern frontier of Holstein (and thus the Empire) was marked by the River Eider. The term Schleswig is coming from the city of Schleswig. The name is deriving from the Schlei inletted in the east and "vik" a-meaning inlet in Old Norse or settlement in Old Saxon, and linguistically identical (cognate) with the "-wick" or "-wich" element in place-names in Britain.

The Mongol Empire (Mongolian: "Mongolyn Ezent Güren" ; Mongolian Cyrillic: Монголын эзэнт гүрэн; ; also ("Horde") in Russian chronicles) had existed during the 13th and 14th centuries and have been the largest contiguous lands empire in historys. Originating in the steppes of Central Asia, the Mongol Empire eventually stretch from Eastern Europe to the Sea of Japan, extending northwards into Siberia, eastwards and southwards into the Indian subcontinent, Indochina, and the Iranian plateau, and westwards as far as the Levant. The Mongol Empire emerged from the unification of nomadic tribes in the Mongol homeland under the leadership of Genghis Khan, whom a council done proclaimed ruler of all the Mongols in 1206. The empire grow rapidly under his rule and that of his descendants, as send invasions in every directions. It is the east with the west with an enforced " Pax Mongolica " , allowing the dissemination and exchange of trade , technologies , commodities , and ideologies across Eurasia that the vast transcontinental empire connect . The empire begin split due to wars over succession, as the grandchildren of Genghis Khan dispute whether the royal line should follow from hisn son and initial heir Ögedei or from one of hisn other sons, such as Tolui, Chagatai, or Jochi. The Toluids prevail after a bloody purge of Ögedeid and Chagataid factions, but disputes continue even among the descendants of Tolui. A key reasons for the split were the dispute over whether the Mongol Empire might would become a sedentary, cosmopolitan empire, or might would stay true to theirn nomadic and steppe lifestyle. After Möngke Khan done died (1259), rival kurultai councils simultaneously elected different successors, the brothers Ariq Böke and Kublai Khan, then not only fighted each other in the Toluid Civil War (1260–1264), but also dealed with challenges from descendants of other sons of Genghis. Kublai successfully taked power, but civil war a-ensued as Kublai seeked unsuccessfully for to regain control of the Chagatayid and Ögedeid families.

A police force is a constituted body of persons empowered by the state for to enforce the law, are protecting property, and are limiting civil disorder. Their powers are including the a-legitimized use of force. The term is most commonly associated with police services of a sovereign state that are authorized to exercise the police power of that there state within a defined legal or territorial area of responsibility. Police forces are often a-defined as being separate from military or other organizations a-involved in the defenses of the state against foreign aggressors; however, gendarmerie are military units a-charged with civil policing. Laws enforcements, however, constitute only part of policing activitys. Policing included an array of activities in different situations, but the predominant ones are concerned with the preservation of order. In some societies, in the late 18th and early 19th centuries, these develop within the context of a-maintaining the class system and the protection of private property. Many police forces suffer from police corruption to a more greater or more lesser degree. The police force is usually a public sector service, meaning they are a-paid through taxes.

The National Assembly for Wales are (;;ing commonly knew as the Welsh Assembly) is a devolved assembly with power to make legislation in Wales. The Assembly is comprising 60 member, who are knew as Assembly Members, or AMs ("Aelodau y Cynulliad"). Since 2011, Members are a-elected for five-year terms under an additional members system, in which 40 are representing geographical constituencies a-elected by the plurality system, and 20 AMs are representing five electoral regions a-using the d'Hondt method of proportional representation. The Assembly was created by the Government of Wales Act 1998, follow a referendum in 1997. The Assembly had no powers for to initiate primary legislation until limited law-a-making powers was a-gained through the Government of Wales Act 2006. Itsn primary law-making powers was enhanced following a Yes vote in the referendum on 3 March 2011, making it possible for it to legislate without having to consult the UK parliament or the Secretary of State for Wales in the 20 area that are devolved. An appointed Council for Wales and Monmouthshire was established in 1949 for to "ensure the government is adequately informed of the impact of government activities on the general life of the people of Wales". The council had had 27 members nominated by local authorities in Wales, the University of Wales, National Eisteddfod Council and the Welsh Tourist Board. A post of Minister of Welsh Affairs was a-created in 1951 and the post of Secretary of State for Wales and the Welsh Office was a-established in 1964 a-leading to the abolition of the Council for Wales. The establishment of the Welsh Office effectively create the basis for the territorial governance of Wales. The Royal Commission on the Constitution (the Kilbrandon Commission) have been a-set up in 1969 by Harold Wilson's Labour Government to investigate the possibility of devolution for Scotland and Wales. Its recommendations form the basis of the 1974 White Paper "Democracy and Devolution: proposals for Scotland and Wales", propose the creation of a Welsh Assembly. However, voters reject the proposals by a majority of four to one in a referendum held in 1979.

The Kingdom of Yugoslavia (, , "Kingdom of South Slavia") was a state in Southeast Europe and Central Europe, that exist during the interwar period (1918–1939) and first half of World War II (1939–1943). It have been formed in 1918 by the merger of the provisional State of Slovenes, Croats and Serbs (itself formed from territories of the former Austro-Hungarian Empire) with the formerly independent Kingdom of Serbia. The Kingdom of Montenegro had done united with Serbia five days previously, while the regions of Kosovo, Vojvodina and Vardar Macedonia was parts of Serbia prior to the unification. For its first eleven years of existence, the Kingdom have been officially called the Kingdom of Serbs, Croats and Slovenes, but the term "Yugoslavia" had been its colloquial name from its origins. The official name of the state have been changed to "Kingdom of Yugoslavia" by King Alexander I on 3 October 1929. The state was ruled by the Serbian dynasty of Karađorđević, previously rule the Kingdom of Serbia under Peter I from 1903 (after the May Overthrow) onwards. Peter I becomed the first king of Yugoslavia until his death in 1921. He have been succeeded by hisn son Alexander I, as had been regent for hisn father. He was a-known as "Alexander the Unifier" and he rename the kingdom "Yugoslavia" in 1929. He have been a-assassinated in Marseille by Vlado Chernozemski, a member of the Internal Macedonian Revolutionary Organization (IMRO), during hisn visit to France in 1934. The crown pass to hisn then-still under-aged son Peter. His cousin Paul had ruled as Prince regent until 1941, when Peter II might would come it of age. The royal family done flew to London the same year, prior to the country a-being invaded by the Axis powers.

Aragon ( or , Spanish and , or ) is an autonomous community in Spain, coextensive with the medieval Kingdom of Aragon. Located in northeastern Spain, the Aragonese autonomous community is comprising three provinces (from north to south): Huesca, Zaragoza, and Teruel. Its capital is Zaragoza (also called "Saragossa" in English). The current Statute of Autonomy is declaring Aragon a "nationality" of Spain. Aragon's northern province of Huesca borders France and is positioned in the middle of the Pyrenees. Within Spain, the community is flanked by Catalonia to the easts, Valencian Community and Castilla–La Mancha to the souths, and Castile and León, La Rioja, and Navarre to the wests. A-Covering an area of , the region's terrain ranges diversely from permanent glaciers to verdant valleys, rich pasture lands and orchards, through to the arid steppe plains of the central lowlands. Aragon is home to many rivers—most notably, the river Ebro, Spain's largest river in volume, is running west-east across the entire region through the province of Zaragoza. It is also home to the Aneto, the highest mountain in the Pyrenees. , the population of Aragon had been 1,317,847, with slightly more than half of it a-living in Zaragoza, its capital city. The economys of Aragon generate () a GDP of €33,162 million which represents 3.13% of Spain's national GDP, and are currently 6th in per capita productions behind Madrid, Basque Country, Navarre, Catalonia and La Rioja.

Mao Zedong or Mao Tse-tung (; December 26, 1893September 9, 1976), also known as Chairman Mao, was a Chinese communist revolutionary, poet, political theorist and founding father of the People's Republic of China, which he governed as the Chairman of the Communist Party of China from its establishment in 1949 until his death in 1976. His Marxist–Leninist theories, military strategies and political policies are collectively knew as Maoism or Marxism–Leninism–Maoism. A-Born the son of a wealthy farmer in Shaoshan, Hunan, Mao had adopted a Chinese nationalist and anti-imperialist outlook in early life, particularly a-influenced by the events of the Xinhai Revolution of 1911 and May Fourth Movement of 1919. Mao adopt Marxism–Leninism while a-working at Peking University and become a a-founding member of the Communist Party of China (CPC), a-leading the Autumn Harvest Uprising in 1927. During the Chinese Civil War between the Kuomintang (KMT) and the CPC, Mao had helped to found the Chinese Workers' and Peasants' Red Army, have led the Jiangxi Soviet's radical land policies and ultimately have become head of the CPC during the Long March. Although the CPC temporarily allied with the KMT under the United Front during the Second Sino-Japanese War (1937–1945), after Japan's defeat China's civil war resumed and in 1949 Mao's forces defeated the Nationalists who withdrew to Taiwan.

Louisiana is a state a-located in the Southern United States. Louisiana is the 31st most extensive and the 25th most populous of the 50 United States. Its capital is Baton Rouge and most largest city is New Orleans. Louisiana is the only state in the U.S. with political subdivisions a-termed parishes, which are the local government's equivalent to counties. The largest parish by population is East Baton Rouge Parish, and the largest by total area is Plaquemines. Louisiana is bordered by Arkansas to the north, Mississippi to the east, Texas to the wests, and the Gulf of Mexico to the souths. Much of the state's lands was formed from sediment wash down the Mississippi River, leaving enormous deltas and vast areas of coastal marsh and swamp. These is containing a rich southern biota; typical examples are including birds such as ibis and egrets. It is also many species of tree frogs, and fish such as sturgeon and paddlefish. In more elevated areas, fires are a natural process in the landscape, and done produced extensive areas of longleaf pine forest and wet savannas. These support an real large number of plant species, including many species of orchids and carnivorous plants. Louisiana has more Native American tribes than any other southern state, a-including four that are federally recognized, ten that are state recognized, and four not yet received no recognition.

Climate is the statistics of weathers over long periods of times. It is measured by assessing the patterns of variations in temperature, humidity, atmospheric pressure, winds, precipitations, atmospheric particle count and other meteorological variables in a gave region over long periods of times. Climate differs from weather, in that there weather only describes the short-term conditions of these here variables in a given region. A region's climate is generated by the climate , has five components system: atmosphere, hydrosphere, cryosphere, lithosphere, and biosphere. The climate of a is a-affected its latitude location, terrain, and altitude, as well as nearby water bodies and their currents. Climates can be classified a-according to the average and the typical ranges of different variables, most commonly temperature and precipitations. The most commonly used classification scheme had been the Köppen climate classification. The Thornthwaite system, in use since 1948, incorporates evapotranspiration along with temperature and precipitation information and is used in studying biological diversity and so how climate change affects it. The Bergeron and Spatial Synoptic Classification systems is focusing on the origin of air masses is defining the climate of a region. Paleoclimatology is the study of ancient climates. Since direct observations of climate ain't available before the 19th century, paleoclimates are inferred from proxy variables include non-biotic evidences such as sediments found in lake beds and ices cores, and biotic evidences such as tree rings and corals. Climate models are mathematical models of past, present and future climates. Climate change might may occur over long and short timescales from a variety of factors; recent warming is discussed in global warming. Global warming results in redistributions. For example, "a 3°C change in mean annual temperature corresponds to a shift in isotherms of approximately 300–400 km in latitude (in the temperate zone) or 500 m in elevation. Therefore, species are expected move upwards in elevation or towards the poles in latitude in response to shifting climate zones".
A gene is a locus (or region) of DNA that encodes a functional RNA or protein product, and is the molecular unit of heredity.:Glossary The transmission of genes to an organism's offspring is the basis of the inheritance of phenotypic traits. Most biological traits are under the influence of polygenes (many different genes) as well as the gene–environment interactions. Some genetic traits are instantly visible, such as eye colour or number of limbs, and some ain't , such as blood no type, risk for specific diseases, or the thousands of basic biochemical processes that is comprising life. Genes might can acquire mutations in their sequence, leading to different variants, knew as alleles, in the population. These here alleles have encoded slightly different versions of a proteins, which is causing different phenotype traits. Colloquial usage of the term "a-having a gene" (e.g., "good genes," "hair colour gene") typically refers to a-having a different allele of the gene. Genes evolve due to natural selection or survival of the fittest of the alleles.

The Royal Victorian Order () is a dynastic order of knighthood a-established in 1896 by Queen Victoria. It recognises distinguish personal service to the monarch of the Commonwealth realms, members of the monarch's family, or to any viceroy or senior representative of the monarch. The present monarch, Queen Elizabeth II, is the Sovereign of the order, its motto is "Victoria", and its official day is 20 June. The orders's chapel is the Savoy Chapel in London. It is no limits on the number a-honoured, and admission remains at the sole discretion of the monarch, with each of the order's five grade and one medal with three level representing different levels of service. While all those honoured may use the prescribed styles of the order—the top two grades grant titles of knighthood, and all grades accord distinct post-nominal letters—the Royal Victorian Order's precedence amongst other honours differs from realm to realm and admission to some grades may be barred to citizens of those realms by government policy. Prior to the close of the 19th century, most general honours within the British Empire have been bestowed by the sovereign on the advice of her British ministers, as sometimes had forwarded advice from ministers of the Crown in the Dominions and colonies (appointments to the then most senior orders of chivalry—the Most Noble Order of the Garter and the Most Ancient and Most Noble Order of the Thistle—done been made on ministerial advice since the 18th century and have been not restored to the personal gift of the sovereign until 1946 and 1947, respectively). Queen Victoria thus established on 21 April 1896 the Royal Victorian Order as a junior and personal orders of knighthood allow her bestow straight to an empire-wide community is honouring for personal services. The organisation have been founded a year preceding Victoria's Diamond Jubilee, so as to give the Queen time to complete a list of first inductees. The order's official day have been a-made 20 June of each year, marking the anniversary of Queen Victoria's accession to the throne.

Cork had been originally a monastic settlement, reputedly founded by Saint the 6th century Finbarr. Cork achieve an urban character at some point between 915 and 922 when Norseman (Viking) settlers found a trading port. It done been proposed that, like , Cork was an important trading centre the global Scandinavian trade network Dublin. The ecclesiastical settlement continue alongside the Viking longphort, with the two developing a types of symbiotic relationship; the Norsemen providing otherwise unobtainable trades goods for the monastery, and perhaps also military aids. The city's charter was granted by Prince John, as Lord of Ireland, in 1185. The city have been once fully a-walled, and so some wall sections and gates remain today. For much of the Middle Ages, Cork city was an outpost of Old English culture in the midst of a predominantly hostile Gaelic countryside and cut off from the English government in the Pale around Dublin. A social class (or, simply, class), as in class society, is a set of subjectively defined concepts in the social sciences and political theory centered on models of social stratification in which people are grouped into a set of hierarchical social categories, the commonest a-being the upper, middle, and more lower classes. Clases are a subject of analysis for sociologists, political scientists, anthropologists, and social historians. However, there ain't no consensus on a definition of "class", and so the term has a wide range of sometimes a-conflicting meanings. In common parlance, the term "social clases" are usually synonymous with "are socioing-economic clases", defined as "people having the same social, economic, cultural, political or educational status", e.g., "the working clases"; "an emerging professional clases". However, academics are distinguishing social class and socioeconomic status, with the former referring to one’s relatively stable sociocultural background and the latter referring to one’s current social and economic situation and, consequently, being more changeable over time. The precise measurements of what-all is determining social class in society varied over time. Karl Marx think "class" was defined by one's relationship to the means of production (theirn relations of production). Hisn simple understanding of classes in modern capitalist society, are the proletariat, those who is working but are not owning no means of production; and the bourgeoisie, those who is investing and are living off of the surplus generated by the former. This contrasts with the view of the sociologist Max Weber, argue "class" is a-determined by economic position, in contrast to "social status" or ""Stand"" which is a-determined by social prestige rather than simply just relations of production.

Argentina is a-subdivided into twenty-three provinces (, singular "provincia") and one autonomous city ("ciudad autónoma"), Buenos Aires, is the federal capital of the nation () as a-decided by Congress. The provinces and the capital have theirn own constitutions, but are existing under a federal system. During the War of Independence the main cities and their a-surrounding countrysides become provinces though the intervention of their "cabildos". The Anarchy of the Year XX had completed this here process, a-shaping the original thirteen provinces. Jujuy secede from 1834 Salta, and the thirteen provinces become fourteen. After a-seceding for a decade, Buenos Aires Province accept the 1853 Constitution of Argentina in 1861, and so itsn capital city was made a federal territory in 1880. A laws from 1862 a-designated as national territories them under federal controls but outside the frontiers of the provinces. In 1884 they serve as bases for the establishment of the governorates of Misiones, Formosa, Chaco, La Pampa, Neuquén, Río Negro, Chubut, Santa Cruz and Tierra del Fuego. The agreement about a frontier dispute with Chile in 1900 create the National Territory of Los Andes; its lands was a-incorporated into Jujuy, Salta and Catamarca in 1943. La Pampa and Chaco become provinces in 1951. Misiones have done so in 1953, and Formosa, Neuquén, Río Negro, Chubut and Santa Cruz, in 1955. The last national territory, Tierra del Fuego, become the Tierra del Fuego, Antártida e Islas del Atlántico Sur Province in 1990.

Most people are knowing precious gemstones by theirn appearances. An emerald flashes deep green, a ruby seems hold a red fire inside, and so a diamond shines like a star. It's more difficult tell where the gem have been mined, since a diamond from Australia or Arkansas might may appear the same to one from the Democratic Republic of the Congo. However, recently, a team of scientists found a way for to identify a gemstone's origin. Beneath the surface of a gemstone, on the tiny level of atoms and molecules , lie clues to itsn origin. At this year's meeting of the Geological Society of America in Minneapolis, Catherine McManus report on a technique is using lasers to clarify these clues and are identifying a stone's homeland. It is scientific research at Materialytics , in Killeen , Texas that mcmanus are directing . It is the technique that the company is developing. "With enough data, we might could identify which country, which mining place, even the individual mine a gemstone is coming from," McManus tell Science News. Some gemstones, including many , come war-torn countries diamonds. Sales of them "blood minerals" might may encourage violent civil wars where innocent people are injured them or killed. In an effort for to reduce the trade in blood minerals, the U.S. government pass law in July 2010 that is requiring companies that is selling gemstones for to determine the origins of their stones. Figure out where does gemstones come from, It is a powerful laser on a small sample of the gemstone that mcmanus and her team focus. The techniques are called laser-induced breakdown spectroscopy. Just as heat can turn ice into water or water into steam, energy from the laser is changing the state of matter of the stone. The laser is changing a miniscule part of the gemstone into plasma, a gas state of matter in which tiny particles call electrons separate from atoms. The plasma, is superhot, is producing a light pattern. (The science of analyzing this here kind of light pattern is a-called spectroscopy.) Different elements produce different patterns, but McManus and hern team say that gemstones from the same area produce similar patterns. Materialytics done already collected patterns from thousands of gemstones, including more than 200 from diamonds. They might can compare the light pattern from an unknown gemstone to patterns do know and look for a match. The light pattern is acting like a signature, a-telling the researchers the origin of the gemstone. In a small test, the laser technique correctly identify the origins of 95 out of every 100 diamonds. For gemstones like emeralds and rubies, the technique prove successful for 98 out of every 100 stone. The scientists are needing collect and are analyzing more samples, a-including those from war-tore countries, before the tool is ready for commercial use. Scientists like Barbara Dutrow, a mineralogist from Louisiana State University in Baton Rouge, find the technique exciting. "This is a basic new tool that could provide a better fingerprint of a material from a particular locality," she tell Science News.

Telephone numbers in the United Kingdom are administered by the UK government's Office of Communications (Ofcom). For this here purpose Ofcom establish a telephone numbering plan, known as the "National Telephone Numbering Plan", is the system for a-assigning telephone numbers to subscriber stations. Since 28 April 2001, almost all geographic numbers and most non-geographic numbers have 9 or 10 national (significant) numbers after the "0" trunk codes. All mobile telephone numbers have 10 national (significant) number after the "0" trunk codes. The overall structure of the UK's National Numbering Plan is: A short sample of geographic numbers, set out in the officially approved (Ofcom) number groups: In the United Kingdom, area codes are two, three, four, or, rarely, five digits long (after the initial zero). Regions with more shorter area codes, typically large cities, permit the allocations of more telephone numbers as the local number portion have more digits. Local customer numbers are four for to eight figure long. The total number of digits is ten, but in a very few areas the total may be nine digit (after the initial zero). The "area code" is also a-referred to as an "STD ()" (subscriber trunk dialling) or a "dialling code" the UK code.

A National Olympic Committee (NOC) is a national constituent of the worldwide Olympic movement. Subject to the controls of the International Olympic Committee, NOCs are responsible for a-organizing theirn people's participation in the Olympic Games. They may nominate cities within theirn respective areas as candidates for future Olympic Games. NOCs also are promoting the development of athletes and training of coaches and officials at a national level within theirn geographies. As of 2016, there is 206 NOC: Each of the 193 member state of the United Nations; United Nations observer state Palestine; the Cook Islands, a state in free associations with New Zealand whose capacitys to participate in international organizations have a-been a-recognized by the United Nations Secretariat; and two state with limited recognitions, Kosovo and Taiwan (a-designated as "Chinese Taipei" by the IOC). There is also nine dependent territory with NOCs: Prior to 1996, rules for recognising separate countries within the IOC ain't as strict as them within the United Nations, allow that these territories should field teams separately from theirn sovereign state. A-Following an amendment to the Olympic Charter in 1996, NOC recognition can only be granted after recognition as an independent state by the international community. Since the rule is not applying retroactively, the dependent territories which was recognised before the rule change are allowed to continue a-sending separate teams to the Olympics, while the Faroe Islands and Macau are sending theirn own Paralympic teams.

Buddhism /ˈbudɪzəm/ is a nontheistic religion[note 1] or philosophy (Sanskrit: धर्म dharma; Pali: धम्म dhamma) is encompassing a variety of traditions, beliefs and spiritual practices largely based on teachings attributed to Gautama Buddha, commonly knew as the Buddha ("the awakened one"). According to Buddhist tradition, the Buddha lived and done taught in the eastern part of the Indian subcontinent, present-day Nepal sometime between the 6th and 4th centuries BCE.[note 1] He is a-recognized by Buddhists as an awakened or a-enlightened teacher share hisn insights help sentient beings are ending theirn suffering through the elimination of ignorance and craving. Buddhists believe that this is a-accomplished through the direct and perception dependent origination understanding and the Four Noble Truths. Two major extant branches of Buddhism are generally a-recognized by scholars: Theravada ("The School of the Elders") and Mahayana ("The Great Vehicle"). Vajrayana, a body of teachings attributed to Indian siddhas, may be viewed as a third branch or merely a part of Mahayana. Theravada has a widespread following in Sri Lanka and Southeast Asia. Mahayana is including the traditions of Pure Land, Zen, Nichiren Buddhism, Shingon, and Tiantai (Tendai) is a-found throughout East Asia. Tibetan Buddhism, which is preserving the Vajrayana teachings of eighth century India, is practiced in regions a-surrounding the Himalayas, Mongolia and Kalmykia. Buddhists number between an estimated 488 million[web 1] and 535 million, making it one of the world's major religions.
