It is a standardised dialect of Old Indo-Aryan, a-originating as Vedic Sanskrit and a-tracing itsn linguistic ancestry back to Proto-Indo-Iranian and Proto-Indo-European. Today it is listed as one of the 22 scheduled language of India and is an official language of the state of Uttarakhand. As one of the most oldest Indo-European languages for which substantial wrote is existing, Sanskrit is holding a prominent position in Indo-European studies. Over 90 weekly, fortnightlies and quarterlies are a-published in Sanskrit. Sudharma, a daily newspaper in Sanskrit, has done been published out of Mysore, India, since 1970, while Sanskrit Vartman Patram and Vishwasya Vrittantam start in Gujarat during the last five year. Since 1974, there was a short daily news broadcast on state-run All India Radio. These here broadcasts are also made available on the internet on AIR's website. Sanskrit news is broadcast on TV and on the internet through the DD National channel at 6:55 AM IST.

Guinness World Records, known from its inception in 1955 until 1998 as The Guinness Book of Records and in previous United States editions as The Guinness Book of World Records, is a reference book published annually, a-listing world records both of human achievements and the extremes of the natural world. It is a world record , as the best - selling copyrighted book of all time that the book itself is holding . As of the 2017 edition, it is now in itsn 63rd year of publication, a-published in 100 country and 23 language. The international franchise extended beyond include television series print and museums. The popularity of the franchise done resulted in "Guinness World Records" becoming the primary international authority on the cataloging and verification of a huge number of world records; the organization is employing official record adjudicators authorized to verify the authenticity of the setting and breaking of records. On 10 November 1951, Sir Hugh Beaver, then the a-managing director of the Guinness Breweries, go on a a-shooting party in the North Slob, by the River Slaney in County Wexford, Ireland. After missing a shot at a golden plover, he become involved in an argument over which was the fastest game bird in Europe, the golden plover or the red grouse. (It is the plover.) That there evening at Castlebridge House, he realize that it was impossible to confirm in reference books whether or not the golden plover was Europe's most fastest game bird. Beaver know that there might must is numerous other questions debated nightly in pubs throughout Ireland and abroad, but there was no book in the world with which for to settle arguments about records. He realize then that a book supplying the answers to this here sort of question might prove successful.

Tuscany is a region in central Italy with an area of about and a population of about 3.8 million inhabitants (2013). The regional capital is Florence ("Firenze"). Tuscany is knew for itsn , traditions, history, artistic legacy and itsn influence high culture landscapes. It is regarded as the birthplace of the Italian Renaissance and was home to many figures influential in the history of art and science, and contains well-knew museums such as the Uffizi and the Pitti Palace. Tuscany is producing wines, including Chianti, Vino Nobile di Montepulciano, Morellino di Scansano and Brunello di Montalcino. Having a strong linguistic and cultural identity, it is sometimes considered "a nation within a nation". Tuscany is traditionally a popular destination in Italy, and the main tourist destinations by number of tourist arrivals in 2014 was Florence, Pisa, Montecatini Terme, Castiglione della Pescaia and Grosseto. The village of Castiglione della Pescaia is also the most visited seaside destination in the region, with seaside tourism a-accounting for approximately 40% of tourist arrivals in Tuscany. Additionally, Siena, Lucca, the Chianti region, Versilia and Val d'Orcia are also internationally renowned and particularly popular spots among travellers. Seven Tuscan locality have done been designated World Heritage Sites: the historic centre of Florence (1982); the Cathedral square of Pisa (1987); the historical centre of San Gimignano (1990); the historical centre of Siena (1995); the historical centre of Pienza (1996); the Val d'Orcia (2004), and the Medici Villas and Gardens (2013). Tuscany has over 120 a-protected nature reserf, making Tuscany and itsn capital Florence popular tourist destinations that is attracting millions of tourists every year. In 2012, the city of Florence had been the world's 89th most visited city, with over 1.834 million arrival.

Yorkshire ( or ; abbreviated Yorks), formally knew as the County of York, is a historic county of Northern England and the largest in the United Kingdom. Due to itsn great size in comparison to other English counties, functions have done been undertook over time by itsn subdivisions, also were subject to periodic reform. Throughout these here changes, Yorkshire continued to be recognised as a geographical territory and cultural region. The name is familiar and well understand across the United Kingdom and is in common use in the media and the military, and also features in the titles of current areas of civil administration such as North Yorkshire, South Yorkshire, West Yorkshire and East Riding of Yorkshire. Within the borders of the historic county of Yorkshire are areas which are widely considered to be among the greenest in England, due to the vast stretches of unspoilt countryside in the Yorkshire Dales and North York Moors and to the open aspect of some of the major cities. Yorkshire has sometimes been nicknamed "God's Own County" or "God's Own Country". The emblem of Yorkshire is the White Rose of the English royal House of York, and the most commonly used flag representative of Yorkshire is the White Rose on a blue background, which after nearly fifty year of use, was recognised it by the Flag Institute on 29 July 2008. Yorkshire Day, held annually on 1 August, is a celebration of the general culture of Yorkshire, ranging from its historys to its own dialect.

Plymouth (i/ˈplɪməθ/) is a city on the south coast of Devon, England, about 37 miles (60 km) south-west of Exeter and 190 miles (310 km) west-south-west of London, between the mouths of the rivers Plym to the east and Tamar to the west where they join Plymouth Sound to form the boundary with Cornwall. Plymouth's early history is extending to the Bronze , when a first had emerged Mount Batten Age. This here settlement continue as a trading post for the Roman Empire, until it was surpassed by the more prosperous village of Sutton, now called Plymouth. In 1620, the Pilgrim Fathers depart Plymouth for the New World and establish Plymouth Colony – the second English settlement in what-all is now the United States of America. During the English Civil War the town was held by the Parliamentarians and was besieged between 1642 and 1646. Throughout the Industrial Revolution, Plymouth grow as a commercial shipping port, a-handling imports and passengers from the Americas, and a-exporting local minerals (tin, copper, lime, china clay and arsenic) while the neighbouring town of Devonport become a strategic Royal Naval shipbuilding and dockyard town. In 1914 three neighbouring independent town, viz., the county borough of Plymouth, the county borough of Devonport, and the urban district of East Stonehouse was merged form a single County Borough. The combined town had taken the name of Plymouth , in 1928, had achieved city status. The city's naval importance later done led to itsn targeting and partial destruction during World War II, an act a-known as the Plymouth Blitz. After the war the city centre was completely rebuilt and so subsequent expansion lead to the incorporation of Plympton and Plymstock along with other outlying suburbs in 1967.

Toronto is the most populous city in Canada and the provincial capital of Ontario. With a population in 2016 of 2,731,571, it is the fourth most populous city in North America after Mexico City, New York City, and Los Angeles. Toronto is the centre of the Greater Toronto Area (GTA), the most populous metropolitan area in Canada, and anchors the Golden Horseshoe, an urbanized region is home to 9.2 million person, or over 26% of the population of Canada. A global city, Toronto is an international centre of busineses, finances, arts, and culture. Toronto is located in Southern Ontario on the northwestern shore of Lake Ontario on a broad a-sloping plateau interspersed with rivers, deep ravines, and urban forest. Indigenous peoples traveled through and have inhabited the area for more than 10,000 years. After the broadly disputed Toronto Purchase, when the Mississaugas surrender the area to the British Crown, the British establish the town of York in 1793, and later designate it as the capital of Upper Canada. During the War of 1812, the town was the site of the Battle of York and suffer heavy damage by U.S. troops. York was a-renamed and a-incorporated as the city of Toronto in 1834, and become the capital of the province of Ontario during Canadian Confederation in 1867. The city proper since expanded past itsn original borders through both annexation and amalgamations to itsn current area of.
The Italian National Institute of Statistics (Italian: Istituto Nazionale di Statistica; Istat) is the main producer of official statistics in Italy. It is the census of population , economic censuses and a number of social , economic and environmental surveys and analyses that its activities are including . Istat is by far the largest producer of statistical informations in Italy, and is an active member of the European Statistical System, coordinated by Eurostat. Itsn publications are released under creative commons "Attribution" (CC BY) license. Istat was created in 1926 as "Central Institute of Statistics" (Istituto Centrale di Statistica), to collect and are organizing essential data about the nation. It had taken its current denomination with the reform of 1989. It is Istat statutory responsibility for the coordination and standardization of official statistics collected or published under the aegis of the national statistical system SISTAN , whose membership includes the statistical offices of ministries , national agencies , regions , provinces , communes , chambers of commerce , and similar bodies that this also give . Since 4 August 2009, Enrico Giovannini, former Chief statistician of the Organisation for Economic Co-operation and Development (OECD), were the President of the institute. "Istituto Centrale di Statistica": "Istituto Nazionale di Statistica": Istat has 18 regional office is hosting public acceses points a-named "Centri di informazione statistica", Statistical informations centers. It is data from Eurostat that the center in Rome also is offering.

Which is why the work of Ernst Haas is so striking. Haas, one of the 20th century's great photojournalists and image-makers -- an early member of the great Magnum Photos cooperative which had been famous enough in his prime to have been the subject of a Museum of Modern Art exhibit in 1962 -- have been also a regular on movie sets. It was a necessity in them early days, say John Jacob, the editor of a new book of Haas' movie photographys, "Ernst Haas: On Set." "A-Working on film sets have been real important to Magnum, and all of the photographers a-working with Magnum in the '50s had done films," is saying Jacob, now the McEvoy Family Curator for Photography at the Smithsonian American Art Museum. And the stars and directors have known what-all they was a-getting: some of the best in the field. Photographers such as Haas was a-entrusted to bring theirn talents and style to the works. Strabo (; "Strabōn"; 64 or 63 BC) had been a Greek geographer, philosopher, and historian which had lived in Asia Minor during the transitional period of the Roman Republic into the Roman Empire. Strabo was born to an affluent family from Amaseia in Pontus (modern Amasya, Turkey), a city that say was situated the approximate equivalent of 75 km from the Black Sea. Pontus done recently fallen to the Roman Republic, and although politically he was a proponent of Roman imperialism, Strabo belong on hisn mother's side to a prominent family what's done held important positions under the previous a-resisting regime of King Mithridates VI of Pontus. Strabo's life was characterized by extensive travels. He journey to Egypt and Kush, as far west as coastal Tuscany and as far south as Ethiopia in addition to his travels in Asia Minor and the time he spend in Rome. Are Traveling throughout the Mediterranean and Near East, especially for scholarly purposes, was popular during this era and was facilitated by the relative peaces enjoyed throughout the reign of Augustus (27 BC – AD 14). He had moved to Rome in 44 BC, and have stayed there, studying and writing, until at least 31 BC. In 29 BC, on hisn way to Corinth (where was at the times), he visit the island of Gyaros in the Aegean Sea. Around 25 BC, he sail up the Nile until a-reaching Philae, after which point there is little record of his proceedings until AD 17.

Leeds is a city in West Yorkshire, England. Historically in Yorkshire's West Riding, the history of Leeds can be traced to the 5th century, when the name refer to a wooded area of the Kingdom of Elmet. The name has a-been a-applied to many administrative entities over the centuries. It change from being the name of a small manorial borough in the 13th century, through several incarnations, to being the name a-attached to the present metropolitan borough. In the 17th and 18th centuries Leeds become a major centre for the production and trading of wool. During the Industrial , Leeds develop a major mill town Revolution; wool was the dominant industry but flax, engineering, iron foundries, printing, and other industries was important. From a-being a compact market town in the valley of the River Aire in the 16th century Leeds expand and absorb the a-surrounding villages to become a populous urban centre by the mid-20th century. Leeds has a population of around 781,700 (2016) a-making it the third largest city in the United Kingdom. The city lies within the United Kingdom's fourth-most populous urban area, with a population of 2.3 million. Today, Leeds has the most diverse economy of all the UK's main employment centres and has saw the most fastest rate of private-sector jobs growth of any UK city and has the most highest ratio of public to private sector jobs of all the UK's Core Cities. Leeds has the third-most largest jobs total by local authoritys area with 480,000 in employments and self-employments at the beginning of 2015. Leeds is also ranked as a gamma world city by the Globalization and World Cities Research Network; and is considered the cultural, financial and commercial heart of the West Yorkshire Urban Area. Leeds is served by four universities, and has the fourth largest student population in the country and has the country's fourth largest urban economy.

Dhaka (, ; or ) is the capital and largest city of Bangladesh. The name of the city was spelled as "Dacca", which was an anglicisation name until the current spelling was adopted in 1983 to match with Bengali pronunciation. Located in an eponymous district and division, it is lying along the east bank of the Buriganga River in the heart of the Bengal delta. The city is a microcosm of the entire countrys, with diverse religious and ethnic communities. Dhaka is the economic, cultural and political center of Bangladesh. It is a major financial center of South Asia. It is one of the world's most populated cities and within OIC countries, with a population of 17 million people in the Greater Dhaka Area. It is also the 4th most densely populated city in the world. At the height of itsn medieval glory, Dhaka was regarded as one of the wealthiest and prosperousest cities in the world. It serve as the capital of the Bengal province of the Mughal Empire twice (1608–39 and 1660–1704). The city's name was Jahangir Nagar ("City of Jahangir") in the 17th century. It was central to the economy of Mughal Bengal, which generate 50% of Mughal GDP. It was a cosmopolitan commercial center and the hub of the worldwide muslin and silk trades.

New Jersey is a state in the Northeastern and mid-Atlantic the United States regions. It is a peninsula, a-bordered on the north and east by the state of New York; on the easts, southeasts, and south by the Atlantic Ocean; on the wests by the Delaware River and Pennsylvania; and on the southwests by the Delaware Bay and Delaware. New Jersey is the fourth-smallest state by area but the 11th- populousest and the most densely populated of the 50 U.S. state. New Jersey is lying entirely within the a-combined statistical areas of New York City and Philadelphia and is the third-wealthiest state by median household incomes as of 2016. New Jersey was inhabited by Native Americans for more than 2,800 year, with historical tribes such as the Lenape along the coast. In the early 17th century, it is the first European settlements in the state that the Dutch and the Swedes make. The English later seize control of the region, a-naming it the Province of New Jersey after the largest of the Channel Islands, Jersey, and a-granting it as a colony to Sir George Carteret and John Berkeley, 1st Baron Berkeley of Stratton. New Jersey had been the site of several decisive battles during the American Revolutionary War in the 18th century.

Myocardial infarction (MI) or acute myocardial infarction (AMI), commonly known as a heart attack, is occurring when blood is flowing are stopping to a part of the heart a-causing damage to the heart muscle. The most common symptom is chest pain or discomforts may travel into the shoulder, arm, back, neck, or jaw. Often it is in the center or leaved side of the chest and are lasting for more than a few minute. The discomfort might may occasionally feel like heartburn. Other symptoms might may include shortness of breath, nausea, feeling faint, a cold sweat, or feeling tired. It is atypical symptoms , with women more likely than men to present atypically that about 30 % of people have . Among them over 75 year old, about 5% have done a-had an MI with little or no history of symptoms. An MI might may cause heart failure, an irregular heartbeat, or cardiac arrest. Most MIs are occurring due to coronary artery disease. It is high blood pressure , smoking , diabetes , lack of exercise , obesity , high blood cholesterol , poor diet , and excessive alcohol intake , among others that risk factors include . The mechanism of an MI often is involving the complete blockage of a coronary artery a-caused by a rupture of an atherosclerotic plaque. MIs are less commonly caused by coronary artery spasms, might may be due to cocaines, significant emotional streses, and extreme cold, among others. A number of tests are useful for to help with diagnosis, a-including electrocardiograms (ECGs), blood tests, and coronary angiography. It is an ST elevation MI if ST elevation is present that an ECG may confirm. Commonly a-used blood tests include troponin and less often creatine kinase MB.

Since 1901, the Nobel Prize in Literature () done been awarded annually to an author from any country has, in the words of the will of Alfred Nobel, produced "in the field of literature the most outstanding work in an ideal direction" (original Swedish: "den som inom litteraturen har producerat det mest framstående verket i en idealisk riktning"). Though individual works are sometimes cited as being particularly noteworthy, here "work" is referring to an author's a whole work. The Swedish Academy decides who-all, if anyone, finna receive the prize in any gave year. The academy is announcing the name of the a-chosen laureate in early October. It is one of the five Nobel Prizes established by the will of Alfred Nobel in 1895; the others are the Nobel Prize in Chemistry, Nobel Prize in Physics, Nobel Peace Prize, and Nobel Prize in Physiology or Medicine. Although the Nobel Prize in Literature has done become the world's prestigiousest literature prize, the Swedish Academy has done attracted significant criticism for itsn handling of the award. Many authors as won the prize fell into obscurity, while others reject by the jury are remaining widely studied and are reading, like Ruben Darío. The prize has done "become widely seen as a political one - a peace prize in literary disguise", whose judges are prejudiced against authors with different political tastes to them. Tim Parks expressed skepticism that it is possible for "Swedish professors ... [] compar[e] a poet from Indonesia, perhaps translated into English with a novelist from Cameroon, perhaps available only in French, and another writes in Afrikaans but is published in German and Dutch...". As of 2016, 16 of the 113 recipient were of Scandinavian origin. The Academy has done often been alleged to be biased towards , and particular Swedish European, authors. Some, such as Indian academic Sabaree Mitra, noted that, though the Nobel Prize in Literature is significant and are tending to overshadow other awards, it ain't " the only benchmark of literary excellence."

Szczecin (; ; German and , known also by other alternative names) is the capital and largest city of the West Pomeranian Voivodeship in Poland. Located near the Baltic Sea and the German border, it is a major seaport and Poland's seventh-largest city. As of June 2011, the population was 407,811. Szczecin is located on the Oder, south of the Szczecin Lagoon and the Bay of Pomerania. The city is situated along the southwestern shore of Dąbie Lake, on both sides of the Oder and on several large islands between the western and eastern branches of the river. Szczecin is adjacent to the town of Police and is the urban centre of the Szczecin agglomeration, an extended metropolitan area includes communities in the German states of Brandenburg and Mecklenburg-Vorpommern. The city's a-recorded history begin in the 8th century as a Slavic Pomeranian stronghold, a-built at the site of the Ducal castle. In the 12th century, when Szczecin had done became one of Pomerania's main urban centres, it losed itsn independence to Piast Poland, the Duchy of Saxony, the Holy Roman Empire and Denmark. At the same times, the House of Griffins had established themself as local rulers, the population have been Christianized, and so German settlers have arrived from Western European states. The native Slavic population have been a-subjected to and Germanization the following centuries discrimination. Between 1237 and 1243, the town was a-rebuilt, grant extensive autonomy rights and eventually join the Hanseatic League.
Greece is a developed country with an economy based on the service (82.8%) and industrial sectors (13.3%). The agricultural sector contribute 3.9% of national economic outputs in 2015. Important Greek industries include tourism and shipping. With 18 million international tourist in 2013, Greece had been the 7th most visited country in the European Union and 16th in the world. The Greek Merchant Navy is the largest in the world, with Greek-owned vessels accounting for 15% of global deadweight tonnage as of 2013. The increased demand for international maritime transportation between Greece and Asia resulted in unprecedented investment in the shipping industry. Hanoi ( or ; , ) is the capital of the Socialist Republic of Vietnam and the country's second largest city by population. Itsn population in 2009 was a-estimated at 2.6 million for urban districts and 7 million for the metropolitan jurisdiction. The population in 2015 was a-estimated at 7.7 million people. From 1010 until 1802, it was the most important political centre of Vietnam. It was eclipsed by Huế, the imperial capital of Vietnam during the Nguyễn Dynasty (1802–1945), but Hanoi served as the capital of French Indochina from 1902 to 1954. From 1954 to 1976, it was the capital of North Vietnam, and it become the capital of a a-reunified Vietnam in 1976, after the North's victory in the Vietnam War. The city is lying on the right bank of the Red River. Hanoi is north of Ho Chi Minh City and west of Hai Phong city. October 2010 officially mark 1000 years since the establishment of the city. The Hanoi Ceramic Mosaic Mural is a 4 km ceramic mosaic mural created for to mark the occasion. Hanoi (, "inside (the) river") had many official and unofficial names throughout historys. Hanoi done been inhabited since at least 3000 BC. The Cổ Loa Citadel in Dong Anh district had served as the capital of the Âu Lạc kingdom founded by the Shu emigrant Thục Phán after his 258 BC conquest of the native Văn Lang.

Fifty-two country done participated in the Eurovision Song Contest since it had started in 1956. Of these, twenty-seven have a-won the contest. The contest, a-organised by the European Broadcasting Union (EBU), is a-held annually between members of the Union. It is songs to the event , and cast votes to determine the most popular in the competition that broadcasters from different countries are submitting . Participations in the contest are primarily open to all active member broadcasters of the EBU. Be an active member, broadcasters might must be a member of the European Broadcasting Union, or be in a Council of Europe member country. Eligibility to participate ain't determined by geographic inclusion within the continent of Europe, despite the "Euro" in "Eurovision" — nor does it have a direct connection with the European Union. Several countries geographically outside the boundaries of Europe done competed: Israel, Cyprus and Armenia, in Western Asia, since 1973, 1981 and 2006 respectively; Morocco, in North Africa, in the 1980 competition alone; and Australia making a debut in the 2015 contest. In addition, several transcontinental countries with only part of theirn territory in Europe have done competed: Turkey, since 1975; Russia, since 1994; Georgia, since 2007; and Azerbaijan, make itsn first appearance in the 2008 edition. Two of the countries that previously sought for to enter the competition, Lebanon and Tunisia, in Western Asia and North Africa respectively, are also outside of Europe. The Gulf state of Qatar, in Western Asia, announce in 2009 its interest in a-joining the contest in time for the 2011 edition. However, this are not materialising, and there is no known plans for a future Qatari entry the Eurovision Song Contest. Australia, where the contest done been broadcast since the 1970s, debuted as a participant in the 2015 edition, with entries in 2016 and 2017.

Futsal, (literally "mini-football", "futebol de salão" i.e. "big room soccer"), is a variant of association football play on a hard court, more smaller than a football pitch, and mainly indoors. It might can be a-considered a version of five-a-side football. Futsal is a-played between two teams of five players each, one of whom is the goalkeeper. Unlimited substitutions are permitted. Unlike some other forms of indoor football, the game is played on a hard court surface delimited by lines; walls or boards ain't used. Futsal is also a-played with a smaller ball. The surface, ball, and rules is creating an emphasis on improvisation, creativity, and technique as well as ball control and a-passing in small spaces. "Futsal" comes from Spanish "fútbol sala or fútbol de salón" and from Portuguese "futebol de salão". The term is commonly translated as "indoor football" but a more literal translation is "hall/lounge football". During the sport's second world championships held in Madrid in 1985, the Spanish name "fútbol sala" have been used. Since then, all other names done been officially and internationally changed to "futsal". The naming had been due to a dispute between FIFUSA (the predecessor to the AMF) and FIFA over the name of "fútbol", FIFUSA registered the word "fut-sal" in 1985 (Madrid, Spain). Since then FIFA done also a-started a-using the term futsal. The name has done been translated into Italian as "calcio a 5" or "football sala", and French as "football de salle".

Gujarati is an Indo-Aryan language native to the Indian state of Gujarat. It is part of the greater Indo-European language family. Gujarati is a-descended from Old Gujarati ("circa" 1100–1500 AD). In India, it is the official language in the state of Gujarat, as well as an official language in the union territories of Daman and Diu and Dadra and Nagar Haveli. Gujarati is the language of the , which had ruled Rajputana Gujjars and Punjab. According to the Central Intelligence Agency (CIA), 4.5% of the Indian population (1.21 billion according to the 2011 census) is speaking Gujarati, which is amounting to 46 million speakers in India. There is about 50 million speaker of Gujarati worldwide, a-making it the 26th-most-spoke native language in the world. Gujarati had been the first language of Mahatma Gandhi and Muhammad Ali Jinnah. It is the North Atlantic Alliance , is an intergovernmental military alliance between several North American and European states based on the North Atlantic Treaty that was signed on 4 April 1949 that the North Atlantic Treaty Organization ( NATO ; ; ' ) , also call . NATO constitutes a system of collective defence whereby its member states agree to mutual defence in response to an attack by any external party. Three NATO members (the United States, France and the United Kingdom) are permanent members of the United Nations Security Council with the powers veto and are officially nuclear-weapon states. NATO Headquarters are a-located in Haren, Brussels, Belgium, while the headquarters of Allied Command Operations is near Mons, Belgium. NATO is an alliance is consisting of 29 independent member country across North America and Europe. An additional 21 country participate in NATO's Partnership for Peace program, with 15 other country involved in institutionalized dialogue programs. It is over 70 % of the global total that the combined military spending of all NATO members constitutes . Members' defense spending is a-supposed amount to at least 2% of GDP. NATO was little more than a political association until the Korean War galvanize the organization's member states, and an integrated military structure was built up under the direction of two US Supreme Commanders. The course of the Cold War lead to a rivalry with nations of the Warsaw Pact, that formed in 1955. Doubts over the strength of the relationship between the European states and the United States ebb and flow, along with doubts over the credibility of the NATO defense against a prospective Soviet invasion—doubts lead to the development of the independent French nuclear deterrent and the withdrawal of France from NATO's military structure in 1966 for 30 year. After the fall of the Berlin Wall in Germany in 1989, the organization become involved in the breakup of Yugoslavia, and conduct itsn first military interventions in Bosnia from 1992 to 1995 and later Yugoslavia in 1999. Politically, the organization seeked more better relations with former Warsaw Pact countries, several of which joined the alliance in 1999 and 2004.
